# Test Design: `scripts/magos_guard.py`

Source: `scripts/magos_guard.py`

Source-SHA256: `83f07146a3f4c8ba82e497b42e867fe08b4738dd6d76a5aa57136653f0b71430`

## Test Strategy

All tests live under `tests/codex/guard/` and run with `pytest` in the lane worktree (runtime host cases stay in the
separate project test worktree described in `AGENTS.md`). Three levels:

1. **Pure unit tests** import `scripts/magos_guard.py` as a module: manifest parsing, normalisation, the glob-intersection
   dynamic programme (checked against a brute-force oracle that enumerates a universe of candidate paths), the decision
   table, message rendering in both languages, the command lexer and detector (including repository redirects and the
   missing-directory rule), the tamper filter, claim parsing and writing, version comparison.
2. **Real-Git integration tests** build throw-away repositories in `tmp_path` with real linked worktrees (committed-ahead
   branch, dirty worktree, untracked file, rename, delete, detached worktree with commits, merge or rebase in progress,
   locked and prunable worktree, remote-tracking branch ahead of the target, bare repository, case collision, unborn
   repository) and drive the CLI in process (`main(argv)`) and as a subprocess. Git runs with an isolated `HOME`,
   `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` and `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM`, and the isolated gitconfig sets `core.longpaths=true` so deep
   temporary paths work on Windows. The sets (`test_gitplan_sets.py`, TC-253 to TC-297, TC-310 to TC-347, TC-350, TC-362 to TC-367, TC-374, TC-375, TC-378, TC-380 to TC-383, TC-386 to TC-388, TC-390, TC-391, TC-398, TC-399, TC-402, TC-403, TC-404, TC-405, TC-406, TC-410, TC-411, TC-412, TC-413, TC-414, TC-415, TC-417, TC-418, TC-419, TC-420, TC-421, TC-422, TC-429, TC-430, whose specs name their lanes by topic) use the same
   repositories with several lane branches and worktrees, a moved or replaced target, claims of other planners, a
   remote-tracking copy of the target, specs written outside the repository, failures injected into `os.replace` and
   `Git.run`, and concurrent processes. Every spec they build carries `requirements`, per lane `serves` and `outcome`, topic ids, the branch
   `agent/<set>/<id>` and a worktree directory named `<repo dir name>-<set>-<id>`; the rows name these fields only where
   they matter.
3. **Host-protocol and process tests** feed Claude, Codex and Hermes PreToolUse payloads (including PowerShell BOM and
   CRLF stdin, `Monitor`, MCP tools the matcher routes, and payloads nested 100,000 levels deep) to `hook`, run the embedded `HOOK_SH` through real Git (`arm`) and directly
   under Git's `sh`, run `run-guard.cmd` through cmd.exe and `sh`, and run two concurrent planners in separate processes.
   Redirect cases run at hook level from three session directories: a directory outside any repository, another clean
   repository, and another repository that holds a lane and a claim for the same name.
   The approval tests (`test_approval.py`, TC-500 to TC-547) feed Claude, Codex and Hermes payloads of user-only commands to
   `hook`, in process and as a subprocess, and run the rewritten command through the real guard with Git's `sh` and, on
   Windows, `powershell.exe`, with stdin closed, so that no console is present.
   The Codex approval tests (`test_mcp_approval.py`, TC-600 to TC-649) drive the MCP approval server (`mcp`) in process
   from a fake host joined to it by two OS pipes, which answers its dialogs, and as a subprocess, and feed Codex payloads
   of user-only and `mcp` commands to `hook`. In their rows "Accept" is the answer with the action `accept` and the
   content `approve` true; a row that names another answer says so.
   The Hermes approval tests (TC-650 to TC-681; in `test_hermes_approval.py`, the plugin's callbacks TC-668 to TC-671 and TC-677 in
   `test_hermes_plugin.py`, the supply-chain row TC-679 and the contract row TC-680 in their own suites) feed Hermes payloads to `hook hermes pre-tool` with and without `--hermes-gate`, in process
   and as a subprocess, run the rewritten command through Git's `sh` (also inside Hermes's `eval` wrapper), and drive
   the plugin's two callbacks through a reimplementation of Hermes's directive aggregation; no Hermes runs. The package
   test of the root `mcp.json` (TC-682) runs its launch through `git` with a stand-in server.

Interpreters: the suites pass on the minimum Python 3.9 and on CPython 3.14 (the `python3` the hooks run under). The
lane gate runs the whole guard suite with `python3` (3.14), the interpreter the hooks run under, because Windows path
handling differs from Python 3.13 on; inside the run, TC-106 compares hook verdicts with the launcher's interpreter and TC-107 replays
the path-sensitive payloads under both `os.path.isabs` behaviours. Every test module starts with `from __future__ import annotations` so it imports on
3.9, and the stdlib-only check falls back to a fixed module list where `sys.stdlib_module_names` is missing. The CI
workflow `.github/workflows/test.yml` runs the guard suite, the contract unittest suite, both contract runners with
`--check` and the version agreement check on windows-latest and ubuntu-latest with Python 3.9 and 3.14, and a
coverage job on windows-latest with Python 3.14 that installs coverage from `.github/requirements/coverage.txt`, runs
the `coverage_commands` of `.quality/qguard.json` and fails when a changed source file has 90 percent line coverage or
less (TC-180). The workflow `.github/workflows/security.yml` runs on pushes and pull requests to main, develop
and develop_codex, on v* tags, and weekly: the replay of the 1.0.2 audit findings (TC-187, TC-188), the hardening and
review suites and the supply-chain contracts (TC-189 to TC-199) on windows-latest and ubuntu-latest with Python 3.14,
and zizmor with `--no-ignores --no-config --collect=all --strict-collection --persona pedantic` and its online audits
over the workflows and `.github/dependabot.yml`. Every CI install takes wheels only from a
requirement file that pins each package by version and sha256 (`.github/requirements` for Python 3.14, which
Dependabot updates after a seven-day cooldown; `.github/requirements-py39`, frozen at the last releases for 3.9). No
test uses an API newer than Python 3.9 (TC-179). Test
helpers start every subprocess with `stdin=DEVNULL` and without inherited console handles, so the suites run under
pytest's default capture on the pymanager 3.14 shim; tests of console-gated commands (`override`, `arm`, `disarm`,
`install`) patch `user_console`. A child that the machine kills (a Windows NTSTATUS exit status of 0xC0000000 and up, also as
`exit code N` in a Git failure the guard reports, or `Out of memory`) is run at most four times in all by the shared
helpers `crashed` and `retried` of `conftest.py`, in these calls: `run` (and so the `guard` fixture), `Repo.git`, `sub` of
`test_gitplan_sets.py`, `raw_git` and the hook subprocesses of TC-106 in `test_hardening_hook.py`, and the branch
creation that the git-layer hook vetoes in TC-302 (`test_hardening_state.py`). Every other subprocess call of the test
files runs once, and a crash that persists still fails the test (TC-399). Fixture repositories are created under a temporary directory. Windows-only cases (the
executable search order, cmd.exe) are skipped elsewhere; POSIX-only cases run on the ubuntu leg. Tests that need a POSIX
`sh` skip where none is found, and no test depends on the name of the temporary directory (TC-181).

The frozen detector corpus (`tests/codex/guard/data/corpus.jsonl`, 1,155 labelled command strings) is replayed with
precision and recall floors; every residual miss and false positive is listed in `data/known_gaps.json`. The
echo-piped-into-sh detector cases and their expected names live in a data file under `tests/codex/guard/data/`, so no
shipped text line has that shape. Coverage is measured with coverage.py (line coverage) through the commands in
`.quality/qguard.json`, for `scripts/magos_guard.py` and for every tracked source of the project (`__init__.py`,
`scripts/sync_hosts.py`), each strictly above 90 percent; the two other files are exercised in process by
`test_project_sources.py`, and `__init__.py` also by `test_hermes_plugin.py` (`scripts/sync_hosts.py` and
`__init__.py` have their own test designs).

## Test Cases

In the table, the character ∣ (U+2223) stands for the shell pipe character, which a table cell cannot hold.

| Case ID | Input / Setup | Expected Outcome | Test Location |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TC-001 | Manifest entries `src/a/`, `src\a\x.py`, `./src//a`, `Src/A`, NFD and NFC spellings | Normalised to one canonical, case-folded NFC form; directory entries also match everything below | tests/codex/guard/test_manifest.py::test_normalisation |
| TC-002 | Entries that are empty, start with `:` or `!`, are absolute (`/x`, `C:x`, UNC), contain `..`, `.git`, `**` inside a segment, match everything (`**`, `*/*`), or exceed 200 entries or 1024 characters | Exit 12 with every reason listed; nothing is written | tests/codex/guard/test_manifest.py::test_invalid_entries |
| TC-003 | `pages/[id].tsx` against its literal path and against `pages/i.tsx` | Brackets are ordinary characters: literal path overlaps; the class reading over-approximates and can only add conflicts | tests/codex/guard/test_manifest.py::test_brackets_are_ordinary_characters |
| TC-004 | Every pair of 63 patterns over a small alphabet (3,969 pairs), including `**` and `?` combinations | Overlap predicate equals the brute-force oracle on every pair | tests/codex/guard/test_manifest.py::test_overlap_matches_oracle |
| TC-005 | `docs/**` vs `**/*.md`, `*.lock` vs `web/package-lock.json`, `src/a` vs `src/ab.py`, a changed file `src/c` against entry `src/c/x.py` | Exact intersection, not a text prefix; a file equal to a proper prefix of the entry's literal part collides | tests/codex/guard/test_manifest.py::test_file_dir_rule |
| TC-006 | Branch ahead of the target by a commit touching `src/a.py`; manifest names `src/a.py` | Deny exit 10, branch named, evidence kind committed, file listed | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_committed_overlap_names_branch |
| TC-007 | Sibling worktree with an unstaged edit, a staged edit and an untracked file inside the manifest | Deny, evidence kind uncommitted with the three paths; the sibling worktree is byte-identical afterwards | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_uncommitted_overlap_and_sibling_untouched |
| TC-008 | Rename and delete committed or staged on another branch; manifest names the old or the new path | Both names are in the footprint; either manifest is denied | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_rename_claims_both_names_and_delete_claims |
| TC-009 | Branch squash-merged into the target (tip not an ancestor); later divergent edit | Stops claiming after the squash; a later divergent edit claims again | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_squash_merged_branch_stops_claiming |
| TC-010 | Merge in progress in a worktree; `git am` state; unreadable operation state | Warning OP_IN_PROGRESS for the first; UNKNOWN OP_METADATA_UNREADABLE for `am` | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_in_progress_operations |
| TC-011 | Remote-tracking branch ahead of the target with no local copy; same name checked out locally; remote copy ahead of the local one | One claimant per branch; warning REMOTE_AS_OF_LAST_FETCH | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_remote_tracking_branch_ahead |
| TC-012 | Target resolution: `--target`, `magos.target`, `origin/HEAD`, `init.defaultBranch`, exactly one of main/master/develop/trunk, several candidates, none, a `--target` that does not resolve | Confidence high or medium (TARGET_INFERRED); several or none gives UNKNOWN TARGET_UNCERTAIN naming `git config magos.target <branch>`; diverged local and remote copies are UNKNOWN | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_target_resolution |
| TC-013 | Dirty primary worktree with 300 deleted paths; manifests under other directories and naming one deleted path | Allow for the unrelated manifest (presence in a checkout claims nothing); deny for the deleted path | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_dirty_primary_worktree_replica |
| TC-014 | `plan` with no claimant overlap | Exit 0, claim file written with all fields, audit line appended, quoted creation command printed | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_plan_registers_claim_and_prints_creation_command |
| TC-015 | Second `plan` with identical entries; with different entries; with different entries and `--amend`; removal-only amend; a new `--worktree` path | No-op exit 0; exit 14; re-judged with own claim excluded; removal-only skips overlap; a different registered path needs `--amend` | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_amend_semantics |
| TC-016 | Two lanes with overlapping declared manifests, lane two planned second | Deny naming lane one as `declared`, planned only; nothing registered for lane two | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_declared_manifests_collide_even_before_any_branch_exists |
| TC-017 | Two processes plan overlapping manifests at the same instant (three rounds); two plan disjoint manifests | Exactly one overlapping planner wins and the loser is denied naming one lane; both disjoint planners win | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_concurrent_planners_one_winner |
| TC-018 | `release` of a planned claim, an unknown claim, a branch with unmerged work, a merged lane with a clean or a dirty worktree, an unreadable claim file | Deleted and audited; exit 0 with a note; exit 16; allowed when clean; exit 16 when dirty; the unreadable file is removed | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_release |
| TC-019 | `list` and `list --drift --json` with planned, active, idle and drifting lanes | States planned, active, idle; drift lists footprint files matched by no entry; exit 0 | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_list_states_and_drift |
| TC-020 | `override` without a console, with a wrong retyped fingerprint, with a right one; replanning; an expired record; a changed repository | Exit 17 without hang; refusal; one-shot record used once; changed target sha voids it | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_override_gate_and_consumption |
| TC-021 | Lock held by another handle past the timeout; claim file replaced while a reader reads; `PermissionError` on replace and on read | Exit 13 (shortened by `MAGOS_LOCK_TIMEOUT`); retries; never a partial claim | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_lock_timeout_and_release |
| TC-022 | Branch names: reserved device names, `D/F` prefix clash with a claim or branch, case collision, forbidden characters, over 100 characters, a leading dash | Rejected with the exact reason before any claim is written | tests/codex/guard/test_plan_store.py::test_branch_name_rules |
| TC-023 | Detector on every lane-creating form in bash, PowerShell and cmd, with `-C`, `-c`, env prefixes, chains, `bash -c`, `-EncodedCommand`, heredocs, aliases, scripts on disk, bundled flags; the echo-piped-into-a-shell forms and their expected names are read from the data file under `tests/codex/guard/data/` | Recognised with kind, names, repository hint and worktree path | tests/codex/guard/test_detector.py::test_lane_creating_forms |
| TC-024 | Detector on negatives: `branch --list`, `branch -d`, `worktree list`, `checkout file`, strings inside `echo`, comments and heredocs | Not recognised as creation | tests/codex/guard/test_detector.py::test_negatives |
| TC-025 | Frozen corpus replay | Precision at least 0.93 and recall at least 0.88 on non-adversarial rows; residual rows listed in `known_gaps.json` | tests/codex/guard/test_detector.py::test_corpus_floors_and_known_gaps |
| TC-026 | Dynamic words (`git $CMD`, backticks, `%x%`) in lane position; a dynamic subcommand; unresolved repository with a lane verb | Flagged dynamic; denied UNPARSEABLE once the creation is guarded | tests/codex/guard/test_hook.py::test_dynamic_names |
| TC-027 | Claude, Codex and Hermes payloads for Bash, PowerShell, terminal, EnterWorktree, Agent isolation, session MCP tools (also `hand_off_to_session`) | Allow is `{}`; deny is the single universal JSON object, exit 0, empty stderr (engagement decided), full conflict list in the message | tests/codex/guard/test_hook.py::test_every_host_gets_the_same_deny_object |
| TC-028 | Named creation with no claim; with a claim; with a claim whose files a sibling dirtied after planning; claim for a different worktree path | NO_MANIFEST with the exact plan command; allow; revalidation denies naming the sibling; path mismatch denied | tests/codex/guard/test_hook.py::test_revalidation_denies_when_a_sibling_dirties_a_planned_file |
| TC-029 | Auto-named creations (agent isolation, `gh pr checkout`, `hand_off_to_session`) with and without another lane | UNPLANNABLE deny when another lane exists or the repository is armed; allow otherwise | tests/codex/guard/test_hook.py::test_auto_named_worktrees_cannot_be_planned |
| TC-030 | Tamper commands: `core.hooksPath`, `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `git config magos.guard`, writes into `.git/hooks`, `override`, `arm`, `install`; ordinary commands that merely name them | PROTECTED deny for the writers; allow for reads, commit messages, greps and heredoc text | tests/codex/guard/test_detector.py::test_protected_denies_real_writers |
| TC-031 | Hook with a crash after a recognised creation; with a crash on a Bash `echo hello` payload sent with no `cwd`, `transcript_path` or other key that could hold a path, so that its raw bytes hold none of the prefilter words (git, worktree, isolation, start_session, EnterWorktree, magos, hookspath, gh, claude, eval, iex in any case) whatever the temporary directory is called; PowerShell stdin with BOM and CRLF; git missing from PATH | Deny JSON; `{}` plus a stderr warning; parsed normally; deny | tests/codex/guard/test_hook.py::test_internal_errors_fail_closed_only_for_lane_creation |
| TC-032 | `arm`, `arm --dry-run`, `arm` twice, with `core.hooksPath` set, with differing worktree hook directories, with a foreign `.magos-prev`; without a console | Files written and printed; idempotent; exit 3 with the reason; exit 17 without a console; nothing written in dry run | tests/codex/guard/test_gitlayer.py::test_arm_writes_the_hook_and_switches |
| TC-033 | In an armed repository: `git branch`, `switch -c`, `checkout -b`, `checkout --orphan`, `update-ref refs/heads/x`, `fetch . main:x`, `worktree add -b` | Each vetoed with the MAGOS message, no branch, no worktree directory, no admin directory left; the same commands pass once a claim exists | tests/codex/guard/test_gitlayer.py::test_creations_without_a_claim_are_vetoed |
| TC-034 | Armed repository: `commit`, `tag`, `stash`, `fetch`, `gc`, `pack-refs`, `switch` to an existing branch, `worktree add --detach`, `worktree add <path> <existing-branch>`, rebase, merge; commits on a lane that existed before arming | All pass untouched | tests/codex/guard/test_gitlayer.py::test_everything_else_passes |
| TC-035 | `HOOK_SH` from a linked worktree and a subdirectory; foreign hook chained with its original stdin; 5,000-line transaction | Same decisions; chained hook sees prepared and committed; linear time | tests/codex/guard/test_gitlayer.py::test_disarm_restores_a_foreign_hook |
| TC-036 | `install --host` with claude, codex and hermes, `--dry-run`, `--home`, console granted | Canonical copy written with the same relative layout and byte-identical to `scripts/magos_guard.py` and `hooks/run-guard.cmd`; generated snippet printed with a quoted launcher; user configuration files untouched | tests/codex/guard/test_gitlayer.py::test_install_copies_the_guard_and_prints_the_snippet |
| TC-037 | `doctor` in healthy, missing-hook, mismatched-hook, hook-bypassed and broken-backstop repositories | Reports problems and exits 1; self-test branch removed; exit 0 when healthy | tests/codex/guard/test_gitlayer.py::test_doctor |
| TC-038 | `run-guard.cmd` through cmd.exe and `sh` with guard exit 0, deny JSON, crash, exit 7, missing script, no Python, `MAGOS_GUARD=off`, a space and CJK directory | Pass-through of stdout and stdin; strict gives exit 2, soft gives warning and exit 0; off gives exit 0 | tests/codex/guard/test_launcher.py::test_cmd_failure_map |
| TC-039 | End-to-end replica of this repository: dirty primary worktree, branches `develop_claude`, `test_claude`, `tests_codex`, new lane for `SKILL.md` and `references/lane-split.md` | Allowed; a second lane for `references/` is denied naming the first | tests/codex/guard/test_pressure.py::test_replica_of_the_users_repository |
| TC-040 | Work-around attempts in an armed repository (variable subcommand, substitution, `bash -c`, `-C`, other tools through the git layer) | Each denied by the host hook or vetoed by the git layer; no new `refs/heads/*` | tests/codex/guard/test_pressure.py::test_workaround_attempts_in_an_armed_repository |
| TC-041 | `hooks/hooks.json` parse, anchored matchers and `shell`; `.gitattributes` endings; launcher executable bit; package contract rows | Valid schema, LF endings; no third file in `.claude-plugin/`; root description within 1024 characters | tests/codex/guard/test_package.py::test_hooks_json_wiring |
| TC-042 | In-process calls of `scripts/sync_hosts.py` and `__init__.py` | Every statement path of both files is exercised for the project coverage gate | tests/codex/guard/test_project_sources.py::test_plan_verify_apply_cycle |
| TC-043 | Committed work on a detached worktree; `worktree add --detach` and `<path> <commit>` | The detached worktree claims its commits; the creation needs a claim keyed `detached/<directory>` | tests/codex/guard/test_review_fixes.py::test_detached_worktrees_need_a_manifest_keyed_by_their_directory |
| TC-044 | Integrated lane without a workspace, fresh branch without a worktree, case-variant branch spelling | Idle claims stop blocking; fresh lanes keep blocking; spelling does not change the state | tests/codex/guard/test_review_fixes.py::test_fresh_branch_without_a_worktree_is_still_a_lane |
| TC-045 | Bare repository with linked worktrees; submodule layout; locked worktree missing on disk | Guarded; the work tree is the repository top; UNKNOWN WORKTREE_UNREADABLE | tests/codex/guard/test_review_fixes.py::test_bare_repository_layout_is_guarded |
| TC-046 | UTF-16 and ANSI manifest files; no username; a `--target` that does not resolve; an exhausted comparison budget | Read or rejected with a clear exit 12; plan still works; exit 11; BUDGET_EXCEEDED | tests/codex/guard/test_review_fixes.py::test_manifest_encodings |
| TC-047 | `plan --worktree` naming another branch's dirty worktree; a detached lane's own dirty worktree; a re-plan spelling the worktree path in another case | The other branch is still a conflict; the detached lane does not conflict with itself; the re-plan is a no-op | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_a_worktree_of_another_branch_at_the_planned_path_is_still_compared |
| TC-048 | 190,000 spaces after `echo`, 18,000 `update-ref` words, 190,000 closing parentheses, 45,000 `-git` options | Detector, tamper filter and alias scan each finish within 5 s | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_worst_case_command_lines_parse_in_linear_time |
| TC-049 | `update-ref --stdin` with a here-string; `echo` text piped into `sh`; `git status && git mkwt ...` where `mkwt` is a worktree alias | Names read; the aliased worktree creation is denied for want of a manifest | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_every_git_call_is_checked_for_an_alias |
| TC-050 | A command longer than 200,000 characters with a parallel lane, then with a claim | Denied as unparseable naming the length; denied as protected (too long to inspect) | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_an_over_long_command_is_denied_with_its_real_reason |
| TC-051 | A planned claimant with declared entries only and an expired deadline; 300 paths with a live deadline | BUDGET_EXCEEDED; all 300 paths compared | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_declared_only_claimants_watch_the_clock |
| TC-052 | A hook call whose shared budget is spent; a named-worktree creation whose check fails in auto mode; a vanished working directory | Deny naming the exhausted budget and no budget left behind; deny for the worktree, allow for a branch-only creation whose check fails the same way; GIT_FAILED, not GIT_MISSING | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_one_hook_call_shares_one_git_budget |
| TC-053 | `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File x.ps1`, `bash -o pipefail x.sh`, `pwsh -NoProfile -File x.ps1` | The script file is read and its creation found | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_the_script_after_option_values_is_read |
| TC-054 | `override --fingerprint ../../x`; `plan --target`; malformed UTF-16 manifests | Exit 2 and no override record; usage error exit 2 (`check --target` works); ENCODING | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_override_wants_the_printed_fingerprint |
| TC-055 | A claim file with only `state`, `branch` and `entry`; a claim with an invalid entry under `list --drift` | Listed as planned and usable by the hook; drift is empty and other lanes get UNKNOWN CLAIM_UNPARSEABLE | tests/codex/guard/test_second_review.py::test_a_claim_without_optional_fields_is_read |
| TC-056 | Session directory outside any repository: `cd /c/...repo && git worktree add ../w -b agent/other` (Git Bash spelling of the repository); session in the repository: `cd /does/not/exist && git worktree add ../w -b agent/other2`; `cd <non-repo dir> && git init x && cd x && git switch -c main2` | The Git Bash spelling is mapped to the repository and the creation is denied for want of a claim; the unmappable POSIX-rooted target is denied; the real non-repository directory is allowed | tests/codex/guard/test_hook.py::test_git_bash_spelled_targets_are_judged_not_waved_through |
| TC-057 | Repository A with an unmerged lane; session directory outside any repository; `git --git-dir=<A>/.git branch x` and `git --git-dir <A>/.git branch x`; then `plan --branch x` in A | Both denied NO_MANIFEST naming `x` and the plan command for A; allowed after the plan; no branch created while denied | tests/codex/guard/test_hardening_hook.py::test_git_dir_redirect_is_judged_in_the_redirected_repository |

## Boundary & Failure Cases

- Empty manifest, 200 and 201 entries, 1024 and 1025 character entries, a manifest file outside and inside a worktree,
  UTF-8 with BOM, UTF-16 and ANSI encodings.
- Repositories with no commits, detached HEAD, bare repository, submodule, no merge base (unrelated history gives the
  warning UNRELATED_HISTORY and the branch is ignored), more than 120 unmerged local branches (UNKNOWN TOO_MANY_CLAIMANTS, engaged in AUTO),
  more than 300 remote-tracking branches (warning REMOTE_TRUNCATED with the count), more than 5,000 paths in one claimant.
- Git failures: missing `git` or an exhausted budget (an error, never "not a repository"), a vanished working directory,
  non-zero exit with and without stderr, timeout, `WinError 1455` retried twice; each ends as UNKNOWN GIT_FAILED,
  GIT_MISSING or BUDGET_EXCEEDED and a denied `plan` or `check`; under `--json` the deny object is printed. Git 2.35 and
  older is reported by `doctor`.
- Locale and encoding: non-ASCII branch and path names with `core.quotepath` forced off, case-insensitive collisions,
  Unicode normalisation forms, trailing dots and spaces, Windows reserved names, Git Bash path spellings (`/c/Users/x`),
  POSIX-rooted spellings that map to no drive (`/does/not/exist`) on interpreters before and after Python 3.13.
- Repository redirects: a relative `--git-dir` before or after `git -C` (joined to the `-C` directory either way), an
  exported relative `GIT_DIR` followed by `cd` (joined to the new directory), a `GIT_DIR` that names a linked worktree's
  `.git` file or a bare repository directory, an empty `GIT_DIR=`, a redirect to a directory that is not a repository, a
  dynamic value, an `unset`, `export -n`, `env -u`, `Remove-Item Env:` or empty PowerShell assignment of `GIT_DIR`
  (ends the redirect), a removal spelling outside that list (the recorded redirect stays), a redirect from a session
  directory outside any repository; `GIT_COMMON_DIR` alone, beside `GIT_DIR`, beside `--git-dir` (which replaces
  `GIT_DIR` only), relative after `-C`, dynamic, missing and removed.
- Definite and possible changes: a `cd` that is the first command of a list, one after `&&` with and without a later
  `;` or `||`, one after `||`, inside each compound command and branch, in a function body and a PowerShell script
  block (defined, called), in a background list and in cmd (where `&` is sequential), behind a program wrapper and
  behind `command` / `builtin`, under `env -C` / `sudo -D` (static and dynamic), in substituted text before and after the
  creation, inside a quoted string; a `fi` inside `$( )` within an outer `if`; redirections after `cd`.
- Scopes: a `cd`, `pushd`, `Set-Location` or redirect change inside a group, inside the first, a middle or the last
  command of a pipeline, inside nested groups, through `eval`, `iex`, `source`, `.` and a PowerShell script; a
  creation inside the group or the evaluated text (judged in the changed state only); a child shell's change (never
  reaches the line); a plain `cd` after a group (one state again); a `)` that closes `$(` inside a group, a quoted `(`
  inside a group and a group inside `$( )` (one stack pairs the parentheses); a group, a background list, a pipeline
  command other than the last and substituted text in bash (no state added), in PowerShell and in a tool whose shell is
  not known (both states); the last pipeline command in bash (both states); exactly eight states (walked) and nine
  (denied when the line creates something or reads a relative script, allowed otherwise).
- Missing directories: `cd` with no argument or a dynamic target (unknown repository), a chain of relative `cd` after a
  missing one, a target that an earlier element of the same `&&` chain creates (`git init`, `git clone`, `git worktree add`,
  `mkdir`, `mkdir -p` of a parent), the same commands joined by `;` (the missing-directory rule), a creation after the
  chain ends, and the accepted over-denial `cd missing && git switch -c x` with nothing creating `missing` (Git would not
  run).
- Tools: an MCP tool the matcher routes by a substring (`read_terminal`, `execute_shell_command`, `Powershell-Tool`) is
  inspected when its command is a string and answered `{}` without parsing when it has none; an MCP session tool without
  a command (still judged); an isolation or session tool that carries a harmless string command (judged as a worktree
  creation as well); a hooked tool without a string command; a Codex argv list; a `~` or `~\` session directory; a
  relative `tool_input.cwd` (joined to the payload `cwd`).
- Claim store: a claim of a newer format without a `branch` line (named by its file), a `v` that is not an integer
  (unreadable, and kept by `release`, also `v=`, `v=1_0`, `v= 1` and a digit outside ASCII), `v=01`, `v=0` and
  `v=1000000`, a claim written with CRLF or a BOM by hand, a claim path that resolves outside `<store>/claims`.
- Sets: specs at and over every limit (12 and 13 lanes, 12 and 13 requirements, 200 and 201 entries, 8,192 and 8,193
  sheet characters, 4 MiB), every JSON shape error (a duplicate key, `NaN`, a number beyond the range of a float in a spec and in a record (TC-364, TC-365), a lone surrogate in a string or a key of a record (TC-412), a boolean `v`, an unknown key, UTF-16, nesting 100,000 deep), a lane
  with no entry, a lane that lists one entry twice in two spellings (TC-327), an invalid set name in each subcommand (TC-331) and one with a lone surrogate (TC-362),
  a lane that owns a file another lane's glob matches, a cycle, a requirement that no lane serves, a lane that
  serves nothing, an unknown requirement or an id with a lone surrogate (TC-363), a lane without an outcome or with one over 300 characters, every refused lane id
  (ordinal, single letter, digits, reserved, uppercase, underscore, 25 characters, leading hyphen), a branch other than
  `agent/<set>/<id>`, a worktree directory that does not end with `-<set>-<id>`, a stale base, a stale revision, a target
  that moved between the spec and the lock, a branch or claim of a lane that already exists, a worktree inside another
  worktree, a record of a newer format and an unreadable one (in `doctor` also under a file name with a lone surrogate or of more than 80 characters, TC-413), a store write that fails on any move (rollback), a lock
  held by another process, a lane removed that holds unmerged work, an integration branch that the target lacks or
  whose worktree is dirty or missing at `set-release` (TC-390, TC-391), a head that is not the branch tip, a file outside
  the manifest at `ready`, a target branch that no longer exists, an interrupted registration (claims without a
  record, a record without claims), a lane claim whose worktree is empty or another path or that carries an override (TC-414, TC-415), a manifest index that answers as `path_hit` does (TC-310) and runs on the budget of the call (TC-429), the drift of a target
  that holds the set's own land, a target commit one ready lane merged, a recorded head or a base that names no object
  (TC-325, TC-326), and, at `integrated`, the changes summary at its bounds (20 commits, 30 files in 15 directories, a
  binary file, a rename, an empty lane, a lane that merged the target tip, a lane of merges only, TC-330), a missing
  target branch, a failing Git call or an exhausted budget (nothing written), a repeated `integrated` update and a lane
  that leaves `integrated`; a lane worktree on a detached HEAD (TC-336, TC-337), missing on disk, locked or not
  (TC-338), a second worktree on a lane branch (TC-339), a record edited so that its base or a head is no object name
  (TC-340), worktree spellings with a leading `//`, a Win32 namespace prefix, a segment that ends in `.` or a space, `~`
  followed by a digit or a `:` after the drive (TC-342, TC-343, TC-366, TC-404, TC-405), the default worktree of a main
  worktree directly below a root or at one, the root of a UNC share included (TC-367, TC-406), the pairwise index
  against a brute force and below a shared top directory (TC-344, TC-345), and a reason or an audit line that cannot be
  written (TC-334, TC-335).
- Install and doctor: version comparison as integer tuples (`1.0.10` is newer than `1.0.9`), a copy without
  GUARD_VERSION (compared by sha256 and treated as older), `install` run from the copy itself (copies nothing),
  a missing `guard/generated/` directory (refresh command printed for every host), a Hermes home from `HERMES_HOME`,
  `%LOCALAPPDATA%` and `~/.hermes`, a Hermes plugin of another or no version, a removed Hermes plugin, a Windows guard home
  with a cmd metacharacter, a Windows Hermes plugin path with one (the entry names the copy), a home with an
  apostrophe, a `$`, a backtick or a `!` (every generated entry runs), a failure on either copied file, `doctor --home`,
  doctor run from the copy (also with a missing `VERSION.json` or a removed source), a copy newer than the running
  guard (no install command), a recorded backstop script of an older version or none, and a PATH with no `py.exe`,
  with only a `.bat` shim and with no interpreter.
- Concurrency: a claim written while a reader lists, two lock holders, a claim file removed between the existence test
  and the read in the git layer (treated as absent, transient deny), two planners passing the early name check before
  either holds the lock, and a claim or record that a reader holds open while a set write moves it into place or
  removes it, an undo's removal included (retried, TC-370 to TC-373), and a removal that fails with another error (raised at once, TC-428).
- Hostile input: huge stdin, invalid UTF-8, JSON that is not an object, unknown tool names, nested wrappers deeper than 3,
  base64 that is not UTF-16LE, command lines built to trigger regex backtracking (linear time), pipe-to-shell inputs
  whose option values could be read as wrappers or assignments (one reading per word, linear time), a line whose walk
  exceeds the budget (here-documents nested in eight states: denied in about a second), a command longer than
  200,000 characters (not inspected, denied once lanes exist), planted `git.exe`, `py.cmd` and `py.bat` in the working
  directory, newlines in `--worktree` and `user.name`, a payload nested 100,000 levels deep, backslash-escaped words in
  bash text, carets in cmd text, backslashes in a shell the hook cannot name, PowerShell backticks and cmd carets in
  tamper words, and a `magos.` word beside a git alias; a program word split by quotes (`g''it`) or escaped in a shell
  the hook cannot name (`g\it`, `g^it`), PowerShell backtick escapes and their special sequences (``gi`t`` is no git),
  `refs/HEADS`, `REFS/heads` and `refs/Heads` spellings, `printf` escapes, quoted names, expansions and unreadable
  sources (a file, another command) of `update-ref --stdin` instructions, `git -C ` repeated
  30,000 times before the alias check (linear time), and the abbreviated `powershell`, `pwsh`, `git rebase`,
  `git update-ref` and `Start-Process` options.
- Regression: a lane that merely checks out files without modifying them never claims them; a denial never creates a
  branch, directory or administrative directory; an override never survives a changed target sha; no hook denies a
  commit, merge, edit or read inside an existing lane; a claim in the session repository never allows a creation in
  another repository; a line that creates nothing is never denied by the state caps; a quoted backslash name is no
  creation; a string command never replaces the judgement a tool's own name calls for; a commit message or a search
  pattern that names a tamper word is never denied; a claim without a set reads and writes byte-identically to 1.0.3; a
  failed set write never leaves a partial claim or record; a changes summary that cannot be computed never writes a record
  or an audit line; a set write that is made is never reported as a failed call; a lane worktree that cannot be read
  never counts as clean; the set's own land never makes its plan stale; a merge commit is never counted as a lane commit;
  `--dry-run` and `set-show` never write.
- MCP approval server: a line over 1 MiB, a line that is no UTF-8 JSON, a batch array, a boolean id, unknown keys, NUL, lone surrogates and 4,097-character strings in the tool arguments, a client without an elicitation form, an Accept without `approve` true (empty, missing or null content, false, a string, a number), the answer Codex gives an empty form under the approval policy `never`, a second call while a dialog is open, a host cancel, no answer within 120 s, end of input during a dialog, a stdout that cannot be written (TC-604 to TC-626, TC-646).
- Here-documents and long texts: a guard run in a here-document or here-string a shell reads, a scope-always finding after a state edit, a text of exactly 200,000, 200,001 and 1,000,000 characters with and without MAGOS, and the data here-documents that must pass (TC-701 to TC-705).
- Reading every part of a command: a guarded finding before an always one in a quoted word, a child shell or the targets of one writer or redirect; the rooted second reading; a `<<` in quotes or a comment; a shell named by a path, in quotes or on the line after the body; a brace expansion or glob up to the slot; the trailing-dot, empty-ANSI-C and 8.3 spellings of the guard word; four nested levels with and without MAGOS; free-threaded, debug and PyPy interpreters; alias values; 8,000 guard-like words (TC-708 to TC-720).
- The approval routes' readings: a Codex command with a `cd`, an environment assignment, another script or an install through a copy; a POSIX backslash in a directory name; a drive-less, missing or `~` directory or interpreter in a Hermes call; a Hermes flow list with a bracket in its comment (TC-721 to TC-725).
- The subcommand of a guard run: a subcommand computed by a variable, a command substitution, a parameter expansion, `$'..'`, an array element, eval, a function's `"$@"`, a PowerShell variable, sub-expression, array element, expression string or splat, a word xargs, parallel or find -exec supplies (also behind `xargs -I` replacing a plain word), an empty slot, a glob, `hook` through the script, the launcher and Start-Process with a redirected stdin, and the false positives that must pass: the guard as an argument of git, grep, cat, wc and `python -m`, a commit message that starts with the script name, `--paths mcp`, an unknown option, `--help` (TC-683 to TC-689).
- Hermes approval: a gate that would not ask (`bypass`, `absent`, `HERMES_YOLO_MODE`, `approvals.mode` off in every spelling Hermes reads as off), a Hermes config that cannot be read (a flow mapping, a repeated key, an alias, a backslash in a double-quoted value, a tab indent, a directory), an `approvals.timeout` below 120, above 900 or no integer, a call without `workdir` and without absolute paths, a `background` or `pty` call, a command that names `--approval`, a mint that cannot write, and in the plugin a guard that cannot start, exits non-zero, prints no JSON object or an answer of no known shape, or runs longer than 25 s, a call without a mark, `MAGOS_GUARD` off and interleaved calls (TC-655 to TC-665, TC-669, TC-670, TC-677).

## Regression Scope

Neighbouring modules and checks that must stay green: `scripts/sync_hosts.py` (its own design is
`.quality/docs/scripts/sync_hosts.py.md`; its package list ships `references/lane-split.md` through the
`references/*.md` glob and never ships the guard), `__init__.py` (`ctx.register_skill` for the skills and exactly two `ctx.register_hook("pre_tool_call", ...)`; its own
test design is `.quality/test-design/__init__.py.test.md`), the package contract suite
`tests/codex/contracts/test_agent_plugin.py` (frontmatter limits, description length within 1024, eight adapters plus
the entry skill, no third file in `.claude-plugin/`, the root `mcp.json` among the package files with one stdio
server whose keys are only those Codex accepts: `type`, `command`, `args`, `env` and `cwd`), the source-contract cases run by
`tests/codex/contracts/run_contract_cases.py --check` (including `README.md` and `README.en.md` pins), the Claude
source-contract cases (`SKILL.md` strings, `README.md`, `references/installation.md` and `references/commands.md` pins,
`references/pressure-tests.md` headings) run by
`tests/claude/scripts/run_claude_contracts.py --check` with the version taken from `plugin.yaml` (its GitSweep cases
`git-sweep/preview-and-help`, `git-sweep/remote-delete-safety`, `git-sweep/one-shot-readiness` and
`git-sweep/push-target` pin the readiness block, the `--push` fast-forward and the hard stops of `/GitSweep`), and the host runtime
cases (`tests/codex/run_host_cases.py`) that require refs and worktree files to be unchanged after read-only commands
(the guard writes only on `plan`, `release`, `override`, `arm`, `disarm`, `install`, `set-plan`, `set-update` and
`set-release`, and never a ref, a branch or a worktree; `check`, `list`, `set-show` and every `--dry-run` write nothing but
the consumption of an `--approval` token, and the host hook writes only approval and denial records, and `mcp` only approval records and what the command it runs writes).

Within the guard, the cases that pin behaviour other parts of 1.0.4 must not disturb: format-1 claims written by
earlier guards stay readable and line 1 stays `state=active` for the git layer, and the claims of a set are format-1
claims with additive keys (TC-055, TC-086, TC-253, TC-302); the plain `plan`, `release`, `list` and `doctor` of a
repository without sets (TC-077, TC-085, TC-094, TC-304); ordinary commands of
every hooked tool still get `{}` (TC-027, TC-066, TC-139, TC-162); the AUTO branch-only allow when its check fails or when
its engagement check fails in a repository without an open claim (TC-052, TC-073); the console gates of `override`, `arm` and `disarm` (TC-020, TC-032), which stay valid at every approval level (TC-528), and the denial of every user-only command by the Hermes entry under `hooks:`, whose snippet stays that of 1.0.7 (TC-651), the Codex hook's denial that names `magos_approve` (TC-511, TC-635), the denial of `mcp` on every host (TC-634), the outputs of Claude Code and Codex, which the Hermes route does not change (TC-675), and the one launch site of the guard, `Git.run` (TC-194); the generated Codex snippet;
and the Git Bash spelling rule on every interpreter (TC-056, TC-106, TC-107).
